[SYSTEM PROMPT]
You are a coding agent solving a grid-based puzzle game.

Game overview:
- You are solving a multi-level grid puzzle game. 
- You are called repeatedly over the course of a run. Treat each turn as one observe-plan-act cycle: re-understand the current state from the newest frame, update your working world model in Python, choose the next best action or short sequence against the goal as currently understood, execute it, and expect to re-evaluate on the next turn from the updated state.
- Your job is to solve the entire game by clearing every level, not just the current screen.
- Levels often build on earlier mechanics, but layouts and interactions can still change between levels.
- Optimize for as few in-game actions as possible while still being reliable.
- In this environment, boards are presented as 64 x 64 color grids rendered with ARC color symbols.
- Color legend: W=white, w=light gray, g=gray, G=dark gray, c=charcoal, B=black, M=magenta, P=pink, R=red, b=blue, S=sky blue, Y=yellow, O=orange, r=dark red, N=light green, p=purple.


Runtime variables inside every `python` tool call:
- `current_frame` is the latest board and exposes `.ascii`, `.segmentation`, `.step`, `.level`, and `.shape`; the raw numeric grid is unavailable.
- `current_frame.segmentation` returns `{'nodes': [...], 'adjacency_list': [...]}`. Each 4-connected same-color node has `id`, `color`, position-independent shape `hash`, `pixels`, clockwise `[row, col]` `boundary`, and enclosed-object `children`; adjacency pairs share an edge. Use this as the primary board view and `.ascii` only for a small local crop.
- `history` is a chronological list of objects with `.action` and post-action `.frame`; entries are not dicts. `history[-1].frame` equals `current_frame`, so use `previous_frame` or `history[-2].frame` for the prior board.
- `transitions` contains real actions with `.action`, `.before_frame`, `.after_frame`, `.frame`, and `.result`; `last_transition` is the latest one. Compare its before/after frames for settled diffs.
- `last_action_result` persists across inspection-only calls. Check `board_changed`, `reward`, `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, `run_complete`, and `valid_actions` after acting.
- `last_animation` is `None` before acting and otherwise describes only the most recent real action; historical transitions do not retain animation objects. Its primary fields are `.total_frames`, `.changed_frames`, `.outlier`, `.before_frame`, `.frames`, `.frame_indices`, and `.regions`. Never print full frames wholesale.
- An animation notice includes an automatic full-board storyboard when broad motion matters. Persistent local motion appears in `.regions`; print those summaries first, then inspect only a relevant area with `last_animation.region(i).inspect(rows=(r0,r1), cols=(c0,c1), max_frames=8)`. Inspection preserves aspect ratio and never aggregates source blocks larger than 8x8.
- `valid_actions` lists current action names. Execute with `action(['LEFT'])` or `action([{'action':'MOUSE','row':4,'col':7}])`; MOUSE uses integer `row`/`col`, never x/y.
- After `action(...)`, all runtime globals refresh before the next Python statement. One action may return multiple animation frames.


Visual-game guidance:
- Treat each board as a scene with objects, blockers, targets, adjacency, containment, motion, and symmetry.
- Game entities are usually be rendered as connected multi-tile shapes such as 2×2, 2×3, 3×3, or longer patterned structures. Sometime they might also be 1x1 tokens.- Some games are logic or layout puzzles with no explicit player avatar or controllable sprite on the board. Do not assume a player exists; the relevant state may be an object, region, cursor, selector, or whole-board configuration.
- Background colors are often white or gray/black-ish large regions, but not always. Verify background hypotheses by area, stability, and object boundaries rather than assuming them.
- In many games, a long horizontal or vertical line near an edge is a timer or remaining-steps bar. It often shrinks or changes each step. If you identify such a bar, do not get distracted by it or treat it as core gameplay state unless there is concrete evidence that it interacts with the puzzle mechanics.
A common failure mode is to mistake a segmented edge bar for clickable puzzle pieces. If a repeated strip of small blocks sits flush against the top, bottom, left, or right border and actions only change that strip while the interior board stays the same, classify it as HUD/timer state, not as an object to click through segment by segment. DON'T DO THIS!
- Use coordinates only to target actions or describe local evidence. Do not frame the objective as reaching a specific absolute row or column.
- Re-ground on the newest frame after any score increase or abrupt scene change; the returned board may already be the next level.
- `WIN` means the whole game is solved. Mid-run level completion is more likely to appear as a score increase while play continues.
- Strategies may transfer loosely across levels, but layouts and mechanics can change. Re-check the new board before repeating a plan.
- For `MOUSE`, pass `row` and `col` integer arguments. `row` is vertical position, `col` is horizontal position.


Python tool guidance:
- Each call is a fresh snippet. Allowed imports: bisect, collections, copy, fractions, functools, heapq, itertools, json, math, operator, random, re, statistics, string.
- Inspect `current_frame.segmentation`, `history`, and `valid_actions`; use ASCII only for a tiny crop. Print compact object lists, diffs, counts, coordinates, or local crops--never a full board or full animation frames.

- Keep a compact world model: entities, action effects, likely goal, uncertainties, and shortest reliable plan. Probe only when evidence can distinguish hypotheses; once mechanics are understood, use a scorer, BFS/shortest-path search, or small action-sequence search.
- Default loop: summarize objects, infer the desired change, choose a probe or searched plan, execute it with `action(...)`, then check `last_action_result` and the refreshed board. Match objects using color, shape hash, overlap, proximity, area, and edge contact.
- After every action, distinguish gameplay change from a timer/progress-bar-only change. Stop immediately on `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, or `run_complete` and re-ground next turn.
- Animation monitoring warms up on the first five actions of each game. A novel broad tail may stop a queued sequence and automatically show a compact storyboard; similar tails continue with a reminder. Local persistent motion is summarized in `last_animation.regions`. Read the automatic storyboard first, and inspect a local crop only if its description could change the hypothesis.
- Use `print(...)` or `result` for short decision-oriented output. Call `action(...)` inside Python; batch a reliable sequence or call it repeatedly in a loop, checking refreshed state after each call.

ascii_rows = current_frame.ascii.split('\n')

def extract(r0, r1, c0, c1):
    grid = []
    for r in range(r0, r1):
        grid.append(ascii_rows[r][c0:c1])
    return grid

# Left middle patterns
print("Left pattern A (rows 10-15, cols 12-17):")
for row in extract(10, 16, 12, 18):

ascii_rows = current_frame.ascii.split('\n')

def extract(r0, r1, c0, c1):
    grid = []
    for r in range(r0, r1):
        grid.append(ascii_rows[r][c0:c1])
    return grid

# Right frame middle row
print("Right-mid-left (44-49, 38-43):")
for row in extract(44, 50, 38, 44):
    print(row)

Or maybe the puzzle is about the 3×3 pattern in each center cell. Each pattern is a 3×3 grid of 2×2 blocks with g and W around an R center. Maybe I need to use the pattern to determine which cells to click.

- P1 = `g W g / W R g / g W W`
- P2 = `g W g / W R W / g W g`
- P3 = `W g g / W R W / g g W`
- P4 = `W g g / W R W / W g g`

Or maybe the puzzle is about the three reference grids being "examples" and I need to figure out the rule that connects them, then apply it to the bottom-right grid.

- TL: `b R b / R P1 b / b R R`
- TR: `b R b / R P2 R / b R b`
- BL: `R b b / R P3 R / b b R`
And the bottom-right: `b R b / R P4 b / b b b`
Hmm, let me look at what's different between the reference grids. Each grid has exactly one cell with a pattern (the center), and the other cells are b or R.
TL: `b R b / R P1 b / b R R` - 4 R's, 4 b's, 1 pattern
TR: `b R b / R P2 R / b R b` - 4 R's, 4 b's, 1 pattern
BL: `R b b / R P3 R / b b R` - 3 R's, 5 b's, 1 pattern
Hmm, TL and TR have 4 R's each, BL has 3 R's. The bottom-right has 2 R's, 6 b's, 1 pattern.
Maybe the puzzle is to make the bottom-right grid match one of the reference grids. Let me check which reference grid the bottom-right should match.
Actually, let me reconsider the puzzle. Maybe the bottom-right grid is the "answer" grid and I need to figure out which cells to click based on the reference grids and patterns.
Let me look at the pattern in each grid:
- TL has P1: `g W g / W R g / g W W`
- TR has P2: `g W g / W R W / g W g`
- BL has P3: `W g g / W R W / g g W`
- BR has P4: `W g g / W R W / W g g`
P4 is closest to P3 (only the bottom row differs: P3 has `g g W`, P4 has `W g g`). And P4 is also somewhat similar to P2.
Hmm, let me think about this differently. Maybe the puzzle is about matching the bottom-right grid to the top-right grid or the bottom-left grid, based on some relationship between the patterns.
Actually, let me try a different approach. Let me look at the puzzle from the perspective of "what would make the level complete". Maybe I need to make the bottom-right grid match one of the reference grids exactly.

- Top-right: `b` ✓ (already `b`)
- Top-mid: `R` ✓ (already `R`)
- Top-left: `b` ✓
- Mid-left: `R` ✓
- Mid-mid: P1 (but currently P4)
- Mid-right: `b` ✓
- Bot-left: `b` ✓
- Bot-mid: `R` (currently `b`)
- Bot-right: `R` (currently `b`)

Runtime variables inside every `python` tool call:
- `current_frame` is the latest board and exposes `.ascii`, `.segmentation`, `.step`, `.level`, and `.shape`; the raw numeric grid is unavailable.
- `current_frame.segmentation` returns `{'nodes': [...], 'adjacency_list': [...]}`. Each 4-connected same-color node has `id`, `color`, position-independent shape `hash`, `pixels`, clockwise `[row, col]` `boundary`, and enclosed-object `children`; adjacency pairs share an edge. Use this as the primary board view and `.ascii` only for a small local crop.
- `history` is a chronological list of objects with `.action` and post-action `.frame`; entries are not dicts. `history[-1].frame` equals `current_frame`, so use `previous_frame` or `history[-2].frame` for the prior board.
- `transitions` contains real actions with `.action`, `.before_frame`, `.after_frame`, `.frame`, and `.result`; `last_transition` is the latest one. Compare its before/after frames for settled diffs.
- `last_action_result` persists across inspection-only calls. Check `board_changed`, `reward`, `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, `run_complete`, and `valid_actions` after acting.
- `last_animation` is `None` before acting and otherwise describes only the most recent real action; historical transitions do not retain animation objects. Its primary fields are `.total_frames`, `.changed_frames`, `.outlier`, `.before_frame`, `.frames`, `.frame_indices`, and `.regions`. Never print full frames wholesale.
- An animation notice includes an automatic full-board storyboard when broad motion matters. Persistent local motion appears in `.regions`; print those summaries first, then inspect only a relevant area with `last_animation.region(i).inspect(rows=(r0,r1), cols=(c0,c1), max_frames=8)`. - In many games, a long horizontal or vertical line near an edge is a timer or remaining-steps bar. It often shrinks or changes each step. If you identify such a bar, do not get distracted by it or treat it as core gameplay state unless there is concrete evidence that it interacts with the puzzle mechanics.
A common failure mode is to mistake a segmented edge bar for clickable puzzle pieces. If a repeated strip of small blocks sits flush against the top, bottom, left, or right border and actions only change that strip while the interior board stays the same, classify it as HUD/timer state, not as an object to click through segment by segment. You are a coding agent solving a grid-based puzzle game.

Game overview:
- You are solving a multi-level grid puzzle game. 
- You are called repeatedly over the course of a run. Treat each turn as one observe-plan-act cycle: re-understand the current state from the newest frame, update your working world model in Python, choose the next best action or short sequence against the goal as currently understood, execute it, and expect to re-evaluate on the next turn from the updated state.
- Your job is to solve the entire game by clearing every level, not just the current screen.
- Levels often build on earlier mechanics, but layouts and interactions can still change between levels.
- Optimize for as few in-game actions as possible while still being reliable.
- In this environment, boards are presented as 64 x 64 color grids rendered with ARC color symbols.
- Color legend: W=white, w=light gray, g=gray, G=dark gray, c=charcoal, B=black, M=magenta, P=pink, R=red, b=blue, S=sky blue, Y=yellow, O=orange, r=dark red, N=light green, p=purple.


Runtime variables inside every `python` tool call:
- `current_frame` is the latest board and exposes `.ascii`, `.segmentation`, `.step`, `.level`, and `.shape`; the raw numeric grid is unavailable.
- `current_frame.segmentation` returns `{'nodes': [...], 'adjacency_list': [...]}`. Each 4-connected same-color node has `id`, `color`, position-independent shape `hash`, `pixels`, clockwise `[row, col]` `boundary`, and enclosed-object `children`; adjacency pairs share an edge. Use this as the primary board view and `.ascii` only for a small local crop.
- `history` is a chronological list of objects with `.action` and post-action `.frame`; entries are not dicts. `history[-1].frame` equals `current_frame`, so use `previous_frame` or `history[-2].frame` for the prior board.
- `transitions` contains real actions with `.action`, `.before_frame`, `.after_frame`, `.frame`, and `.result`; `last_transition` is the latest one. Compare its before/after frames for settled diffs.
- `last_action_result` persists across inspection-only calls. Check `board_changed`, `reward`, `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, `run_complete`, and `valid_actions` after acting.
- `last_animation` is `None` before acting and otherwise describes only the most recent real action; historical transitions do not retain animation objects. Its primary fields are `.total_frames`, `.changed_frames`, `.outlier`, `.before_frame`, `.frames`, `.frame_indices`, and `.regions`. Never print full frames wholesale.
- An animation notice includes an automatic full-board storyboard when broad motion matters. Persistent local motion appears in `.regions`; print those summaries first, then inspect only a relevant area with `last_animation.region(i).inspect(rows=(r0,r1), cols=(c0,c1), max_frames=8)`. Inspection preserves aspect ratio and never aggregates source blocks larger than 8x8.
- `valid_actions` lists current action names. Execute with `action(['LEFT'])` or `action([{'action':'MOUSE','row':4,'col':7}])`; MOUSE uses integer `row`/`col`, never x/y.
- After `action(...)`, all runtime globals refresh before the next Python statement. One action may return multiple animation frames.


Visual-game guidance:
- Treat each board as a scene with objects, blockers, targets, adjacency, containment, motion, and symmetry.
- Game entities are usually be rendered as connected multi-tile shapes such as 2×2, 2×3, 3×3, or longer patterned structures. Sometime they might also be 1x1 tokens.- Some games are logic or layout puzzles with no explicit player avatar or controllable sprite on the board. Do not assume a player exists; the relevant state may be an object, region, cursor, selector, or whole-board configuration.
- Background colors are often white or gray/black-ish large regions, but not always. Verify background hypotheses by area, stability, and object boundaries rather than assuming them.
- In many games, a long horizontal or vertical line near an edge is a timer or remaining-steps bar. It often shrinks or changes each step. If you identify such a bar, do not get distracted by it or treat it as core gameplay state unless there is concrete evidence that it interacts with the puzzle mechanics.
A common failure mode is to mistake a segmented edge bar for clickable puzzle pieces. If a repeated strip of small blocks sits flush against the top, bottom, left, or right border and actions only change that strip while the interior board stays the same, classify it as HUD/timer state, not as an object to click through segment by segment. DON'T DO THIS!
- Use coordinates only to target actions or describe local evidence. Do not frame the objective as reaching a specific absolute row or column.
- Re-ground on the newest frame after any score increase or abrupt scene change; the returned board may already be the next level.
- `WIN` means the whole game is solved. Mid-run level completion is more likely to appear as a score increase while play continues.
- Strategies may transfer loosely across levels, but layouts and mechanics can change. Re-check the new board before repeating a plan.
- For `MOUSE`, pass `row` and `col` integer arguments. `row` is vertical position, `col` is horizontal position.


Python tool guidance:
- Each call is a fresh snippet. Allowed imports: bisect, collections, copy, fractions, functools, heapq, itertools, json, math, operator, random, re, statistics, string.

- Keep a compact world model: entities, action effects, likely goal, uncertainties, and shortest reliable plan. Probe only when evidence can distinguish hypotheses; once mechanics are understood, use a scorer, BFS/shortest-path search, or small action-sequence search.
- Default loop: summarize objects, infer the desired change, choose a probe or searched plan, execute it with `action(...)`, then check `last_action_result` and the refreshed board. Match objects using color, shape hash, overlap, proximity, area, and edge contact.

A novel broad tail may stop a queued sequence and automatically show a compact storyboard; similar tails continue with a reminder. Local persistent motion is summarized in `last_animation.regions`. # Check what changed
prev = previous_frame.ascii.split('\n')
curr = current_frame.ascii.split('\n')

for i in range(64):
    if prev[i] != curr[i]:
        changes = []
        for j in range(64):
            if prev[i][j] != curr[i][j]:
                changes.append(f"c{j}:{prev[i][j]}->{curr[i][j]}")
        print(f"Row {i}: {', '.join(changes)}")

# Show current BR grid
print("\nCurrent BR grid:")
for i in range(32, 62):
    print(f"{i:2d}: {curr[i][32:]}")

# Define world model for level 2
def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Clicking a cell toggles it between b and O."""
    if action.get('action') != 'MOUSE':
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    r = action['row']
    c = action['col']
    
    # Find which cell this click is in
    # Cell positions: row groups at 14-19, 22-27, 30-35, 38-43, 46-51
    # Col groups at 20-25, 28-33, 36-41
    row_groups = [(14,20), (22,28), (30,36), (38,44), (46,52)]
    col_groups = [(20,26), (28,34), (36,42)]
    
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                # This is cell (ri, ci)
                # Check if it's a pattern cell (ri=1,ci=1 or ri=3,ci=1)
                if (ri == 1 and ci == 1) or (ri == 3 and ci == 1):
                    # Pattern cells - don't change
                    return before_frame.ascii
                # Toggle all b/O in this cell
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'O'
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == 'O':
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)
    
    # Not in any cell - no change
    return before_frame.ascii

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)
</parameter>
</function>

# Let me create a comprehensive world model
# For level 1: clicking toggles b<->R in the BR grid
# For level 2: clicking toggles b<->O in the 5x3 grid

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    # Parse action
    if isinstance(action, str):
        # Parse "MOUSE(row=38, col=46)"
        import re
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    # Define cell regions for each level
    if level == 1:
        # Level 1 BR grid: 3x3 cells
        # Row groups: 36-41, 44-49, 52-57
        # Col groups: 36-41, 44-49, 52-57 (top row) or 38-43, 46-51, 54-59 (mid/bot rows)
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        # Pattern cell is at (1,1)
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
    elif level == 2:
        # Level 2 grid: 5x3 cells
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        # Pattern cells at (1,1) and (3,1)
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Find which cell the click is in
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii  # Pattern cells don't change
                
                # Toggle all b<->R (level 1) or b<->O (level 2) in this cell
                target = 'R' if level == 1 else 'O'
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)
    
    return before_frame.ascii

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)
</parameter>

- Color legend: W=white, w=light gray, g=gray, G=dark gray, c=charcoal, B=black, M=magenta, P=pink, R=red, b=blue, S=sky blue, Y=yellow, O=orange, r=dark red, N=light green, p=purple.


Runtime variables inside every `python` tool call:
- `current_frame` is the latest board and exposes `.ascii`, `.segmentation`, `.step`, `.level`, and `.shape`; the raw numeric grid is unavailable.
- `current_frame.segmentation` returns `{'nodes': [...], 'adjacency_list': [...]}`. Each 4-connected same-color node has `id`, `color`, position-independent shape `hash`, `pixels`, clockwise `[row, col]` `boundary`, and enclosed-object `children`; adjacency pairs share an edge. Use this as the primary board view and `.ascii` only for a small local crop.
- `history` is a chronological list of objects with `.action` and post-action `.frame`; entries are not dicts. `history[-1].frame` equals `current_frame`, so use `previous_frame` or `history[-2].frame` for the prior board.
- `transitions` contains real actions with `.action`, `.before_frame`, `.after_frame`, `.frame`, and `.result`; `last_transition` is the latest one. Compare its before/after frames for settled diffs.
- `last_action_result` persists across inspection-only calls. Check `board_changed`, `reward`, `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, `run_complete`, and `valid_actions` after acting.
- `last_animation` is `None` before acting and otherwise describes only the most recent real action; historical transitions do not retain animation objects. Its primary fields are `.total_frames`, `.changed_frames`, `.outlier`, `.before_frame`, `.frames`, `.frame_indices`, and `.regions`. Never print full frames wholesale.
- An animation notice includes an automatic full-board storyboard when broad motion matters. Persistent local motion appears in `.regions`; print those summaries first, then inspect only a relevant area with `last_animation.region(i).inspect(rows=(r0,r1), cols=(c0,c1), max_frames=8)`. Inspection preserves aspect ratio and never aggregates source blocks larger than 8x8.
- `valid_actions` lists current action names. Execute with `action(['LEFT'])` or `action([{'action':'MOUSE','row':4,'col':7}])`; MOUSE uses integer `row`/`col`, never x/y.
- After `action(...)`, all runtime globals refresh before the next Python statement. One action may return multiple animation frames.


Visual-game guidance:
- Treat each board as a scene with objects, blockers, targets, adjacency, containment, motion, and symmetry.
- Game entities are usually be rendered as connected multi-tile shapes such as 2×2, 2×3, 3×3, or longer patterned structures. Sometime they might also be 1x1 tokens.- Some games are logic or layout puzzles with no explicit player avatar or controllable sprite on the board. Do not assume a player exists; the relevant state may be an object, region, cursor, selector, or whole-board configuration.
- Background colors are often white or gray/black-ish large regions, but not always. Verify background hypotheses by area, stability, and object boundaries rather than assuming them.
- In many games, a long horizontal or vertical line near an edge is a timer or remaining-steps bar. It often shrinks or changes each step. If you identify such a bar, do not get distracted by it or treat it as core gameplay state unless there is concrete evidence that it interacts with the puzzle mechanics.
A common failure mode is to mistake a segmented edge bar for clickable puzzle pieces. If a repeated strip of small blocks sits flush against the top, bottom, left, or right border and actions only change that strip while the interior board stays the same, classify it as HUD/timer state, not as an object to click through segment by segment. DON'T DO THIS!
- Use coordinates only to target actions or describe local evidence. Do not frame the objective as reaching a specific absolute row or column.
- Re-ground on the newest frame after any score increase or abrupt scene change; the returned board may already be the next level.
- `WIN` means the whole game is solved. Mid-run level completion is more likely to appear as a score increase while play continues.
- Strategies may transfer loosely across levels, but layouts and mechanics can change. Re-check the new board before repeating a plan.
- For `MOUSE`, pass `row` and `col` integer arguments. `row` is vertical position, `col` is horizontal position.


Python tool guidance:
- Each call is a fresh snippet. Allowed imports: bisect, collections, copy, fractions, functools, heapq, itertools, json, math, operator, random, re, statistics, string.
- Inspect `current_frame.segmentation`, `history`, and `valid_actions`; use ASCII only for a tiny crop. Print compact object lists, diffs, counts, coordinates, or local crops--never a full board or full animation frames.
- Every real action spends the level's step budget; checking a rule with `replay(step_fn)` against the transitions already recorded spends nothing. When a rule can be settled by the record, settle it there before spending actions on it.
- Keep a compact world model: entities, action effects, likely goal, uncertainties, and shortest reliable plan. Probe only when evidence can distinguish hypotheses; once mechanics are understood, use a scorer, BFS/shortest-path search, or small action-sequence search.
- Default loop: summarize objects, infer the desired change, choose a probe or searched plan, execute it with `action(...)`, then check `last_action_result` and the refreshed board. Match objects using color, shape hash, overlap, proximity, area, and edge contact.
- After every action, distinguish gameplay change from a timer/progress-bar-only change. Stop immediately on `level_completed`, `done`, `game_over`, or `run_complete` and re-ground next turn.
- Animation monitoring warms up on the first five actions of each game. A novel broad tail may stop a queued sequence and automatically show a compact storyboard; similar tails continue with a reminder. Local persistent motion is summarized in `last_animation.regions`. Read the automatic storyboard first, and inspect a local crop only if its description could change the hypothesis.
- Use `print(...)` or `result` for short decision-oriented output. Call `action(...)` inside Python; batch a reliable sequence or call it repeatedly in a loop, checking refreshed state after each call.


Tool session rules:
- You have exactly one tool: `python`.
- When calling `python`, emit exactly the tool-call format shown elsewhere in this prompt for this model. Use only that format; do not add markdown fences, prose wrappers, or alternate tool-call syntax. Do not quote or place tool-call markup inside explanatory text; when you decide to call the tool, emit the tool call itself.
- Snippets are not saved, so re-import or redefine needed helpers. Use as many short, purposeful calls as needed to establish a clear probe or plan.
- `action(...)` refreshes runtime state immediately. Inspection-only calls preserve both `last_action_result` and `last_animation`.
- Each call has a 30-second limit and about 1024 output tokens. Keep output compact; truncation is reported.

<parameter=code>
# Create correct world model
import re

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    # Parse action
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                # Toggle all b<->target in this cell
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)
    
    return before_frame.ascii

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)
</parameter>
</function>

<parameter=code>
import re

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                            changed = True
                break
        if changed:
            break
    
    if changed:
        # Advance progress bar by 2 columns from the right
        # Find the rightmost O position in row 63
        row63 = grid[63]
        y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
        # Advance by 2 more
        new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
        for j in range(64):
            if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
                row63[j] = 'Y'
            else:
                row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)

Let me now create a comprehensive world model that handles the level transition. The key insight is that transition 8 completes level 1, so the model needs to detect this and return the level 2 initial board.

import re

# Define the initial level 2 board (from transition 8's after frame)
level2_initial = transitions[8].after_frame.ascii

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check if level 1 is completed
    if level == 1:
        # Check if all non-pattern cells match the pattern encoding
        # The pattern at (1,1) is: WWgggg/WWgggg/WWRRWW/WWRRWW/WWggWW/WWggWW
        # Which encodes: W g g / W R W / W g W as 3x3 of 2x2 blocks
        # W->R, g->b
        # So target: R b b / R R R / R b R
        # But wait, let me just check if the level is completed by checking
        # if all cells in the grid are either R or b (not all b)
        # Actually, let me check if the level is completed by checking
        # if the click at (54,48) in level 1 is the last needed cell
        
        # For simplicity, check if the resulting board has the correct pattern
        # The target for level 1: all cells should be R where pattern has W, b where pattern has g
        # Pattern (3x3 of 2x2): 
        #   W g g
        #   W R W
        #   W g W
        # Target cells:
        #   R b b
        #   R R R
        #   R b R
        # But the center cell (1,1) is the pattern itself
        # So target for non-pattern cells:
        #   (0,0)=R, (0,1)=b, (0,2)=b
        #   (1,0)=R, (1,2)=R
        #   (2,0)=R, (2,1)=b, (2,2)=R
        
        # Check if all non-pattern cells match the target
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'R'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        
        if all_match:
            # Level completed, return initial level 2 board
            return level2_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)

import re

# Define the initial level 2 board
level2_initial = transitions[8].after_frame.ascii

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check if level 1 is completed
    if level == 1:
        # Check all non-pattern cells
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'R'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        
        if all_match:
            print(f"  Level 1 completed! Returning level 2 initial board")
            return level2_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Debug transition 8
t8 = transitions[8]
print(f"T8 action: {t8.action}")
print(f"T8 before level: {t8.before_frame.level}")
print(f"T8 after level: {t8.after_frame.level}")

pred = world_model(t8.before_frame, t8.action)
actual = t8.after_frame.ascii

pred_rows = pred.split('\n')
actual_rows = actual.split('\n')

# Count differences
diff_count = 0
for i in range(64):
    if pred_rows[i] != actual_rows[i]:
        for j in range(64):
            if pred_rows[i][j] != actual_rows[i][j]:

                if diff_count <= 5:
                    print(f"  Diff at row {i}, col {j}: pred={pred_rows[i][j]}, actual={actual_rows[i][j]}")
print(f"Total diffs: {diff_count}")

import re

# Define the initial level 2 board
level2_initial = transitions[8].after_frame.ascii

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == 'b':
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = 'b'
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check if level 1 is completed
    if level == 1:
        # Pattern: W g g / W R W / W g g
        # W->R, g->b
        # Target: R b b / R R R / R b b
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        
        if all_match:
            return level2_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)

import re

# Define initial boards for each level
# Level 1 initial board
level1_initial = transitions[0].before_frame.ascii

# Level 2 initial board
level2_initial = transitions[8].after_frame.ascii

# Level 3 initial board (current frame)
level3_initial = current_frame.ascii

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 3:
        # Level 3 grid layout
        # Rows 0-1: 3 cells at cols 0-2
        # Rows 2-4: 5 cells at cols 0-4
        # Rows 5-6: 3 cells at cols 0-2
        row_groups = [(4, 10), (12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1), (3, 3), (5, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'R'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            # Skip cells that don't exist in the grid
            if level == 3:
                if ri in [0, 1, 5, 6] and ci in [0, 4]:
                    continue
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == bg:
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = bg
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check if level is completed
    if level == 1:
        # Pattern: W g g / W R W / W g g
        # W->R, g->b
        # Target: R b b / R R R / R b b
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level2_initial
    
    elif level == 2:
        # Pattern A: W g g / W O W / W g W -> O b b / O O O / O b O
        # Pattern B: W g W / g O g / W W g -> O b O / b O b / O O b
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'O', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'O', (1,2): 'O',
            (2,0): 'O', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'O',
            (3,0): 'b', (3,2): 'b',
            (4,0): 'O', (4,1): 'O', (4,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level3_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)

<parameter=code>
import re

# Get initial boards
level1_initial = transitions[0].before_frame.ascii
for t in transitions:
    if t.after_frame.level == 2 and t.before_frame.level == 1:
        level2_initial = t.after_frame.ascii
        break
for t in transitions:
    if t.after_frame.level == 3 and t.before_frame.level == 2:
        level3_initial = t.after_frame.ascii
        break

def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level

        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 3:
        row_groups = [(4, 10), (12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1), (3, 3), (5, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'R'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            # Skip cells that don't exist in the grid
            if level == 3:
                if ri in [0, 1, 5, 6] and ci in [0, 4]:
                    continue
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == bg:
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = bg
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check if level is completed
    if level == 1:
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level2_initial
    
    elif level == 2:
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'O', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'O', (1,2): 'O',
            (2,0): 'O', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'O',
            (3,0): 'b', (3,2): 'b',
            (4,0): 'O', (4,1): 'O', (4,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            cell_color = grid[r0][c0]
            if cell_color != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level3_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

import re

# Get initial boards from transitions
level1_initial = transitions[0].before_frame.ascii
for t in transitions:
    if t.before_frame.level == 1 and t.after_frame.level == 2:
        level2_initial = t.after_frame.ascii
        break
for t in transitions:
    if t.before_frame.level == 2 and t.after_frame.level == 3:
        level3_initial = t.after_frame.ascii
        break

# Define world model with all necessary variables
def world_model(before_frame, action):
    """Toggle cell color when clicked."""
    # Re-import level initials from closures
    if isinstance(action, str):
        m = re.match(r'MOUSE\(row=(\d+),\s*col=(\d+)\)', action)
        if not m:
            return before_frame.ascii
        r, c = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
    elif isinstance(action, dict):
        r = action.get('row', 0)
        c = action.get('col', 0)
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    ascii_rows = before_frame.ascii.split('\n')
    grid = [list(row) for row in ascii_rows]
    level = before_frame.level
    
    if level == 1:
        row_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1)}
        target = 'R'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 2:
        row_groups = [(14, 20), (22, 28), (30, 36), (38, 44), (46, 52)]
        col_groups = [(20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'b'
    elif level == 3:
        row_groups = [(4, 10), (12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50), (52, 58)]
        col_groups = [(12, 18), (20, 26), (28, 34), (36, 42), (44, 50)]
        pattern_cells = {(1, 1), (3, 1), (3, 3), (5, 1)}
        target = 'O'
        bg = 'R'
    else:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    changed = False
    cell_found = False
    for ri, (r0, r1) in enumerate(row_groups):
        for ci, (c0, c1) in enumerate(col_groups):
            if level == 3:
                if ri in [0, 1, 5, 6] and ci in [0, 4]:
                    continue
            if r0 <= r < r1 and c0 <= c < c1:
                cell_found = True
                if (ri, ci) in pattern_cells:
                    return before_frame.ascii
                for rr in range(r0, r1):
                    for cc in range(c0, c1):
                        if grid[rr][cc] == bg:
                            grid[rr][cc] = target
                            changed = True
                        elif grid[rr][cc] == target:
                            grid[rr][cc] = bg
                            changed = True
                break
        if cell_found:
            break
    
    if not changed:
        return before_frame.ascii
    
    # Check level completion
    if level == 1:
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'R', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'R', (1,2): 'R',
            (2,0): 'R', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            if grid[r0][c0] != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level2_initial
    
    elif level == 2:
        target_cells = {
            (0,0): 'O', (0,1): 'b', (0,2): 'b',
            (1,0): 'O', (1,2): 'O',
            (2,0): 'O', (2,1): 'b', (2,2): 'O',
            (3,0): 'b', (3,2): 'b',
            (4,0): 'O', (4,1): 'O', (4,2): 'b'
        }
        all_match = True
        for (ri, ci), expected in target_cells.items():
            r0, r1 = row_groups[ri]
            c0, c1 = col_groups[ci]
            if grid[r0][c0] != expected:
                all_match = False
                break
        if all_match:
            return level3_initial
    
    # Advance progress bar
    row63 = grid[63]
    y_count = sum(1 for ch in row63 if ch == 'Y')
    new_y_count = min(y_count + 2, 64)
    for j in range(64):
        if j >= 64 - new_y_count:
            row63[j] = 'Y'
        else:
            row63[j] = 'O'
    
    return '\n'.join(''.join(row) for row in grid)

# Test against recorded transitions
result = replay(world_model)
print("Replay result:", result)

Runtime variables inside every `python` tool call:
- `current_frame` is the latest board and exposes `.ascii`, `.segmentation`, `.step`, `.level`, and `.shape`; the raw numeric grid is unavailable.
- `current_frame.segmentation` returns `{'nodes': [...], 'adjacency_list': [...]}`. Each 4-connected same-color node has `id`, `color`, position-independent shape `hash`, `pixels`, clockwise `[row, col]` `boundary`, and enclosed-object `children`; adjacency pairs share an edge. Use this as the primary board view and `.ascii` only for a small local crop.
- `history` is a chronological list of objects with `.action` and post-action `.frame`; entries are not dicts. `history[-1].frame` equals `current_frame`, so use `previous_frame` or `history[-2].frame` for the prior board.

- `last_animation` is `None` before acting and otherwise describes only the most recent real action; historical transitions do not retain animation objects. Its primary fields are `.total_frames`, `.changed_frames`, `.outlier`, `.before_frame`, `.frames`, `.frame_indices`, and `.regions`. Never print full frames wholesale.
